### 🤖 AI Intelligence Summary
> Here's an executive summary of Amrutanjan Health Care Limited's FY2025-26 annual report submission:  Amrutanjan Health Care Limited reported strong performance in FY2025-26, achieving 11% revenue growth and 25% profit growth (before exceptional items), primarily driven by its pain management and women's hygiene segments. A material impact is the significant capital investment in a new sanitary napkin manufacturing plant, aimed at bolstering future growth in the women's hygiene category, alongside continued portfolio diversification and digital distribution expansion. The key red flag is the ongoing "severe regulatory hurdles" negatively impacting the electrolyte drink category, which has been a setback to its growth for the past three years.

## Page 4

Focused Execution.  
Stronger Momentum.
From a single balm in 1893, Amrutanjan’s journey across generations has been shaped by 
one enduring belief: relief must be trusted and effective. Its Corporate Promise, ‘Pure Healthy 
Essence’, brings together science and naturalness to create efficacious products rooted in 
Ayurveda and evolving consumer needs.
Over the years, Amrutanjan has expressed this promise 
in ways relevant to the needs and opportunities of each 
period. Today, that promise is being carried forward 
through a sharper focus on execution, strengthening 
distributor and sub-distributor networks, and expanding 
rural outreach through van initiatives.
It is this clarity of focus that creates stronger momentum, 
allowing the Company to build on its established 
strengths while extending its relevance across categories. 
From its pain-management heritage, Amrutanjan has 
expanded into congestion care, women’s hygiene, 
rehydration and adjacent personal-care categories. 
During FY2026, pain management and women’s hygiene 
recorded double-digit gross sales growth, while new 
launches extended the portfolio across personal care, 
ortho pain and nasal care.
This year’s theme, Focused Execution, Stronger 
Momentum. encapsulates this progression, shining light 
on the Company’s tangible strengths and consistency 
in execution.
AMRUTANJAN HEALTH CARE LIMITED

## Page 5

CORPORATE OVERVIEW
A Promise in Motion:  
Trusted Relief, Timeless Care
In 1893, a yellow jar of pain balm quietly entered Indian households and with 
it, began a legacy of trust and care. Amrutanjan became more than a remedy; it 
became a fixture on shelves and in suitcases alike, carried across generations as a 
dependable answer to everyday aches.
That iconic balm was never just a product; it was a symbol 
of thoughtful care: rooted in Ayurveda, shaped by nature, 
delivered with integrity. From this humble beginning grew 
a legacy built on reliability, simplicity, and the quiet art of 
everyday wellness.
Since its inception, the Company has expanded its 
portfolio across pain management, Women’s hygiene, 
rehydration, congestion relief, and preventive health, each 
category shaped by the same principles of purity, efficacy, 
and accessibility. We carry the promise to evolve without 
ever losing the essence of who we are, a brand born from 
relief, growing with purpose, and focused on the well-
being of those we serve.
Vision 
We wish to grow at 
33% CAGR
Mission
To enhance the living standards of customers 
through innovative products
Values
	
ǵ Commitment towards the interests of all stakeholders 
– customers, employees, shareholders, and community.
	
ǵ We never compromise on our ethics, and this is 
reflected in all our actions.
Brand Manifesto
	
ǵ We stand for individual and family well-being.
	
ǵ Our expertise lies in combining science with 
naturalness, and we have been honing this since 1893.
	
ǵ We are committed to creating offerings that help 
people get rid of the pain.
	
ǵ Our products will be efficacious in providing the relief 
that our customers seek.
	
ǵ We endeavour to delight our customers through the 
experience we provide.
	
ǵ We strive to provide a culture that accepts new ideas, 
embraces change, and rejects bureaucracy and small-
mindedness.
	
ǵ The products we make are pure and natural.
	
ǵ We are against using chemicals like Diclofenac, which 
are harmful and have side effects that impact the larger 
environment as well.
	
ǵ We will stay loyal to our roots of Ayurveda, 
naturalness, and usage of herbs in our products.

PRODUCT PORTFOLIO

Amrutanjan's diversified portfolio reflects a focused approach to meeting evolving wellness 
needs through relevant categories, formats and products. Spanning pain and congestion 
management, women’s hygiene, rehydration and adjacent personal-care categories, the 
portfolio combines established brands with targeted innovation to support the Company’s 
continued growth.

Expanding with Focus
Innovation remains central to Amrutanjan’s approach to portfolio development. 
In FY2026, the Company entered complementary categories and introduced new 
products and formats across personal care, ortho pain and nasal care. These 
launches reflect its focus on addressing a wider range of consumer needs while 
building on the strength of its established brands. Across categories,  
the above products and ranges were introduced.

## Page 9

Message From the Chairman
Dear Valued Owners,
 It is a pleasure to share the progress our 
company made during the past year. The 
year under review saw revenue growth 
of 11% and profit growth of 25% (before 
exceptional item). The Pain management 
and Women’s hygiene categories were the 
drivers of growth led by the power brands of 
Amrutanjan and Comfy. 
Your company posted strong performance as a result 
of the fundamentals that continue to guide our core 
strategy for sustainable growth - Strengthening power 
brands, distribution expansion, product innovation and 
operational excellence via digitization.
Your company was able to adopt to the rise in emerging 
digital channels of commerce with presence of its brands 
across E-commerce and Quick Commerce accounts. 
One of the significant events in the company was 
the investment in a world class manufacturing plant 
to produce sanitary napkins. Your company will be 
commissioning the plant in the 1st quarter of 2027. The 
plant will provide design and supply chain control to the 
brand and enable further growth in market share. 
Our company operates in Health and Hygiene space. 
The Indian Health and Wellness market is valued $15B 
(source IQVIA) and the OTC segment at 60%, is the 
largest contributor to this market. The Indian consumer 
is increasingly embracing self-care and taking greater 
responsibility for managing everyday health needs. 
This structural shift is creating a compelling long-
term opportunity for the OTC healthcare industry and 
reinforces our confidence in the growth potential of 
our business.
The opportunity, however, is accompanied by challenges. 
A dynamic regulatory environment and the time 
required to build consumer awareness, trust, and brand 
consideration demand disciplined execution and sustained 
investment. In OTC healthcare, enduring brands are built 
through consistent innovation, scientific credibility, broad 
availability, and meaningful consumer engagement.
We believe our Company is well positioned to capitalize 
on these trends. With a clear strategy, trusted brands, 
expanding distribution, and strong execution capabilities, 
we are confident in our ability to meet evolving consumer 
needs while delivering sustainable, profitable growth and 
creating long-term value for our shareholders.
In the OTC space, the successful expansion of brand 
Amrutanjan into modern format of Roll On ensures 
the brand remains relevant to the changing needs of 
the consumer. Amrutanjan Roll On is the leader in 
the category and continues to grow revenue at a high 
double-digit pace. This year the company also relaunched 
its brand Jiffy to cater to those consumers who seek 
allopathic pain relief solutions- the brand is available in 
spray and oral analgesic formats.
Our vision is to evolve the brand beyond its heritage 
in headache relief to become a trusted partner in 
comprehensive pain management, addressing a broader 
spectrum of everyday pain, including body pain, muscle 
pain, and joint care. 
In the Hygiene space, your company focuses on Women’s 
Hygiene with brand Comfy of Period care products. 
The brand which was launched in 2013, occupies a 
4th position Nationally in the mass pad segment. This 
year the brand expanded into Comfy Night pads and 
plans are under way to launch Period Panties and Panty 
liners. This will provide a broad portfolio of period 
care products catering to various consumer segments 
and usage occasions. The broader vision for Comfy is 
to be a Women’s hygiene and wellness brand.  The 
company launched Comfy Silky Smoothe and Close and 
Clean Women’s razors to enter the fast growing under 
penetrated women’s hair removal market. Amrutanjan 
Plastry bandages were also introduced thereby 
expanding our OTC portfolio into the wound care market.
The Electrolyte drink category is globally on the rise due 
to increased daily temperatures and also rise in active 
life styles. This is a serious category that saves lives as 
electrolyte imbalances can lead to dangerous health 
conditions, The company operates in this category with 
two brands – Amrutanjan Electro+ ORS and Amrutanjan 
Enerlyte. The category experienced severe regulatory 
hurdles for the past 3 years and this has been a setback 
to growth. But we remain committed to building this 
category over the long term via increased consumer 
education and expansion in distribution touch points and 
product innovation.
The company recognizes the increasing share of digital 
commerce and also how online channels shape consumer 
behavior in brand discovery and purchase decisions. 
To capitalize on this shift, the company has Invested 
in human capital in areas of digital marketing and 
E-commerce. Our successful launch of brand Amrutanjan 
on Amazon USA and Amazon India is a testament to our 
increasing capability in this area of digital commerce. 
In general, we are expanding our investment in human 
capital in the areas of Learning and growth, Sales and 
marketing, IT and Supply Chain.
Digitization is recognized as an important strategic pillar 
for Amrutanjan and we are proud to say that 70% of 
critical processes are automated with plans to make this 
close to 100%. 
The company’s focus is to increase distribution touch 
points beyond our strong markets in South and East. Our 
stated goal of achieving 100K direct chemist outlets will 
be achieved in FY 27 and we wish to further increase 
overall reach and availability of our brands beyond our 
traditional strong markets.
Our CSR program continues to evolve in the areas of 
Education, Women’s health and animal rights. We are 
proud to say we allocated more than the required budget 
this year and continue to be a responsible corporate 
citizen of the Nation and the world.
We are committed to executing our stated plans and 
create long term wealth for our stock holders.
S. Sambhu Prasad 
Chairman & Managing Director
AMRUTANJAN HEALTH CARE LIMITED

INITIATIVES AND MILESTONES
A Year of Focused Execution: 
Initiatives and Milestones
During FY2026, Amrutanjan advanced its growth priorities through focused execution 
across distribution, brand-building, portfolio expansion and people capabilities. The 
Company deepened penetration in core markets through rural distribution and low-unit-
price packs, invested in low-contribution markets and continued to broaden Comfy into a 
women’s wellness brand.
These priorities were supported by an expanding sales and distribution network, high-impact media investments, 
stronger in-store visibility, consumer trial initiatives and new launches across OTC and personal-care categories.

## Page 11

Growth Milestones Across Brands
Pain Management
The overall pain-management category recorded 10% gross sales growth in FY2026, with the 
headache and body-ache categories growing by 8% and 16%, respectively.
Headache (Brand Amrutanjan)
Body Ache (Brand Amrutanjan)
◊	 Yellow Balm, White Balm, and Head Roll-
On contributed to the category’s gross 
sales growth.
◊	 Head-roll-on sales grew by 21%.
◊	 Larger SKUs of 27.5 ml and 50 ml grew by 
17%, while the 8 ml pack grew by 4%.
◊	 Head Roll-On growth was supported by 
wider distribution and stronger offtake.
◊	 Growth was led by Back Pain Roll-On and 
Maha Strong. 
◊	 Maha Strong grew by 22%, while Back 
Pain Roll-On grew by 12%.
Congestion (Brand Relief)
The Relief range recorded 9% gross sales growth in FY2026, supported by:
◊	 16% growth in Relief Inhaler 
◊	 15% growth in Relief Rubs
Women’s Hygiene (Brand Comfy)
Comfy recorded 19% gross sales growth in FY2026, supported by:
◊	 44% growth in the Comfy XL variant 
◊	 42% growth in the 18-unit Value Pack
◊	 An increase in the XL variant’s 
contribution to overall Comfy 
sales from 31% in FY2025 to 37% 
in FY2026

CORPORATE SOCIAL RESPONSIBILITY
A Promise of Care.  
A Purpose Beyond Business.
At Amrutanjan Health Care Limited, giving back is a natural extension of the care that 
has defined the Company since its inception.
The Company’s approach to CSR is guided by the same 
principles that have shaped Amrutanjan over the years: 
compassion, accountability, and a commitment to long-
term well-being. Aligned with its mission of enhancing 
living standards, the Company extends this commitment 
beyond its customers to communities at large. Its CSR 
initiatives focus on education, women’s empowerment, 
preventive healthcare, socio-economic development, 
environmental sustainability, and animal welfare, with

Distribution  
Expansion
Cost Environment
Digital Transformation
Demand Outlook and 
Brand Strategy
Capacity Expansion
Amrutanjan continues to widen distribution in new 
markets while deepening penetration in existing 
strongholds. In line with this, 44,000 new chemist outlets 
were added during FY2026, with further plans to add 
1,00,000 chemists by FY2027.
On the cost front, the Company anticipated COGS 
inflation in FY2026 on account of the ongoing Middle East 
conflict impacting crude prices and global supply chains, 
a challenge it has partially mitigated through calibrated 
price increases.
On the digital front, the Company has digitised 68% of its 
core processes, and with the full rollout of the Distributor 
Management System (DMS) and SAP TM (Transport 
Module)  in FY2026, the Company is aiming to reach 
approximately 85% digitisation. This will reinforce a high 
level of automation, control, and efficiency across its 
organisational systems.
Amrutanjan remains confident about the demand 
environment heading into FY2027, supported by 
differentiated value propositions across its portfolio. As 
part of its brand strategy, the Company plans to relaunch 
its congestion-relief brand, Relief, during the year. 
Looking ahead, the new Sanitary Pad manufacturing plant 
is scheduled to commence operations in May of FY2027, 
which is further expected to enhance the profitability of 
the Comfy brand.

7, 2023, issued by the Securities and Exchange Board 
of India (SEBI), Companies are allowed to hold Annual 
General Meeting (AGM) through video conferencing 
('VC') or Other Audio Visual Means ('OAVM') without 
the physical presence of members at a common venue. 
Hence, in compliance with the above Circulars, the 
89th AGM of the Company is being conducted through 
VC or OAVM facility. Hence the members can attend 
and participate in the ensuing AGM through VC/
OAVM. The deemed venue for the 89th AGM shall be 
the Registered Office of the Company.
2.	
In compliance with various Circulars issued by MCA 
and SEBI, the Notice calling the AGM has been 
uploaded on the website of the Company at www.
amrutanjan.com. The Notice can also be accessed 
from the websites of the Stock Exchanges i.e. National 
Stock Exchange of India Limited (NSE) and BSE 
Limited at www.nseindia.com and www.bseindia.com 
respectively. The AGM Notice is also disseminated on 
the website of CDSL (agency for providing the Remote 
e-Voting facility and e-voting system during the AGM) 
i.e. www.evotingindia.com.
3.	
In compliance with various Circulars issued by MCA 
and SEBI, the Notice calling the AGM and Annual 
Report for the Financial Year 2025-26 are being sent 
only through electronic mode to those Members whose 
email addresses are registered with the Company/ 
Depositories. For any communication, the shareholders 
may also send requests to the Company’s investor mail 
id: shares@amrutanjan.com.
4.	
A member entitled to vote at the AGM is entitled to 
appoint proxy to attend and vote on his/her behalf 
and the proxy need not be a member. In terms of 
MCA Circulars, since physical attendance of Members 
has been dispensed with, there is no requirement 
of appointment of proxies. Accordingly, facility of 
appointment of proxies by Members under Section 
105 of the Companies Act, 2013 ('the Act') will not be 
available for the 89th AGM and hence the Proxy Form 
and Attendance Slip are not annexed to the Notice. 
However, bodies corporates/institutional members 
(other than individuals, HUF, NRI, etc) are entitled to 
appoint authorised representatives as the members 
to attend the AGM through VC/OAVM, participate 
thereat and cast their votes through e-voting.
5.	
Facility of joining the AGM through VC/OAVM shall 
open 30 minutes before the time scheduled for the 
AGM and the Members can join the AGM by following 
the Instructions mentioned in this Notice. The facility 
of participation at the AGM through VC/OAVM will 
be made available to at least 1000 members on first 
come first served basis. This will not include large 
Shareholders (Shareholders holding 2% or more 
shareholding), Promoters, Institutional Investors, 
Directors, Key Managerial Personnel, the Chairpersons 
of the Audit Committee, Nomination and Remuneration 
Committee and Stakeholders Relationship Committee, 
Auditors etc. who are allowed to attend the AGM 
without restriction on account of first come first 
served basis.

be counted for the purpose of reckoning the quorum 
under Section 103 of the Companies Act, 2013.

27.	 VOTING THROUGH ELECTRONIC MEANS
	
1.	
Pursuant to the provisions of Section 108 of the 
Companies Act, 2013 read with Rule 20 of the 
Companies (Management and Administration) 
Rules, 2014 (as amended) and Regulation 
44 of SEBI (Listing Obligations & Disclosure 
Requirements) Regulations 2015 (as amended), 
and MCA Circulars dated April 08, 2020, April 13, 
2020 and May 05, 2020 the Company is providing 
facility of remote e-voting to its Members 
in respect of the business to be transacted 
at the AGM. For this purpose, the Company 
has entered into an agreement with Central 
Depository Services (India) Limited (CDSL) for 
facilitating voting through electronic means, as 
the authorized e-Voting’s agency. The facility of 
casting votes by a member using remote e-voting 
as well as the e-voting system on the date of the 
AGM will be provided by CDSL.
	
2.	
The attendance of the Members attending the 
AGM through VC/OAVM will be counted for 
the purpose of ascertaining the quorum under 
Section 103 of the Companies Act, 2013.
	
3.	
Pursuant to MCA Circular No. 14/2020 dated 
April 08, 2020, the facility to appoint proxy to 
attend and cast vote for the members is not 
available for this AGM. However, in pursuance of 
Section 112 and Section 113 of the Companies 
Act, 2013, representatives of the members such 
as the President of India or the Governor of a State 
or body corporate can attend the AGM through 
VC/OAVM and cast their votes through e-voting.
THE INTRUCTIONS FOR SHAREHOLDERS FOR 
E-VOTING AND JOINING VIRTUAL MEETINGS 
ARE AS UNDER:
Step 1 : Access through Depositories CDSL/NSDL e-Voting 
system in case of individual shareholders holding shares in 
demat mode.
Step 2 : Access through CDSL e-Voting system in case of 
shareholders holding shares in physical mode and non-
individual shareholders in demat mode.
(i)	
The voting period begins on September 20, 2026 
from 09.00 A.M and ends on September 22, 2026 
at 05.00 P.M. During this period shareholders of the 
Company, holding shares either in physical form or in 
dematerialized form, as on the cut-off date (record 
date) of (September 11, 2026) may cast their vote 
electronically. The e-voting module shall be disabled 
by CDSL for voting thereafter.
(ii)	
Shareholders who have already voted prior to the 
meeting date would not be entitled to vote at the 
meeting venue.
(iii)	 Pursuant to SEBI Circular No. SEBI/HO/CFD/CMD/
CIR/P/2020/242 dated December 9, 2020, under 
Regulation 44 of Securities and Exchange Board of India 
(Listing Obligations and Disclosure Requirements) 
Regulations, 2015, listed entities are required to 
provide remote e-voting facility to its shareholders, in 
respect of all shareholders’ resolutions. However, it 
has been observed that the participation by the public 
non-institutional shareholders/retail shareholders is at 
a negligible level.
	
Currently, there are multiple e-voting service providers 
(ESPs) providing e-voting facility to listed entities in 
India. This necessitates registration on various ESPs 
and maintenance of multiple user IDs and passwords 
by the shareholders.
	
In order to increase the efficiency of the voting process, 
pursuant to a public consultation, it has been decided to 
enable e-voting to all the demat account holders, by 
way of a single login credential, through their demat 
accounts/ websites of Depositories/ Depository 
Participants. Demat account holders would be 
able to cast their vote without having to register 
again with the ESPs, thereby, not only facilitating 
seamless authentication but also enhancing ease and 
convenience of participating in e-voting process.
	
Step 1 : Access through Depositories CDSL/NSDL 
e-Voting system in case of individual shareholders 
holding shares in demat mode.
(iv)	 In terms of SEBI circular no. SEBI/HO/CFD/CMD/
CIR/P/2020/242 dated December 9, 2020 on 
e-Voting facility provided by Listed Companies, 
Individual shareholders holding securities in demat 
mode are allowed to vote through their demat 
account maintained with Depositories and Depository 
Participants. Shareholders are advised to update their 
mobile number and email Id in their demat accounts in 
order to access e-Voting facility.
Pursuant to abovesaid SEBI Circular, Login method for e-Voting and joining virtual meetings for Individual shareholders 
holding securities in Demat mode CDSL/NSDL is given below:
Type of shareholders
Login Method
Individual Shareholders 
holding securities in

Depository
1)	 Users who have opted for CDSL Easi / Easiest facility, can login through their existing user 
id and password. Option will be made available to reach e-Voting page without any further 
authentication. The users to login to Easi / Easiest are requested to visit CDSL website www.
cdslindia.com and click on login icon & My Easi New (Token) Tab.
2)	 After successful login the Easi / Easiest user will be able to see the e-Voting option for eligible 
companies where the evoting is in progress as per the information provided by company. On 
clicking the evoting option, the user will be able to see e-Voting page of the e-Voting service 
provider for casting your vote during the remote e-Voting period or joining virtual meeting 
& voting during the meeting. Additionally, there is also links provided to access the system 
of all e-Voting Service Providers, so that the user can visit the e-Voting service providers’ 
website directly.
3)	 If the user is not registered for Easi/Easiest, option to register is available at CDSL website 
www.cdslindia.com and click on login & My Easi New (Token) Tab and then click on registration 
option.
4)	 Alternatively, the user can directly access e-Voting page by providing Demat Account Number 
and PAN No. from a e-Voting link available on www.cdslindia.com home page. The system 
will authenticate the user by sending OTP on registered Mobile & Email as recorded in the 
Demat Account. After successful authentication, user will be able to see the e-Voting option 
where the evoting is in progress and also able to directly access the system of all e-Voting 
Service Providers.
Individual Shareholders 
holding securities  
in demat mode with 
NSDL Depository
1)	 If you are already registered for NSDL IDeAS facility, please visit the e-Services website of 
NSDL. Open web browser by typing the following URL: https://eservices.nsdl.com either on a 
Personal Computer or on a mobile. Once the home page of e-Services is launched, click on the 
'Beneficial Owner' icon under 'Login' which is available under ‘IDeAS’ section. A new screen 
will open. You will have to enter your User ID and Password. After successful authentication, 
you will be able to see e-Voting services. Click on 'Access to e-Voting' under e-Voting services 
and you will be able to see e-Voting page. Click on company name or e-Voting service provider 
name and you will be re-directed to e-Voting service provider website for casting your vote 
during the remote e-Voting period or joining virtual meeting & voting during the meeting.
2)	 If the user is not registered for IDeAS e-Services, option to register is available at https://
eservices.nsdl.com. Select 'Register Online for IDeAS 'Portal or click at https://eservices.nsdl.
com/SecureWeb/IdeasDirectReg.jsp
3)	 Visit the e-Voting website of NSDL. Open web browser by typing the following URL: https://
www.evoting.nsdl.com/ either on a Personal Computer or on a mobile. Once the home page 
of e-Voting system is launched, click on the icon 'Login' which is available under ‘Shareholder/
Member’ section. A new screen will open. You will have to enter your User ID (i.e. your sixteen-
digit demat account number hold with NSDL), Password/OTP and a Verification Code as shown 
on the screen. After successful authentication, you will be redirected to NSDL Depository site 
wherein you can see e-Voting page. Click on company name or e-Voting service provider name 
and you will be redirected to e-Voting service provider website for casting your vote during the 
remote e-Voting period or joining virtual meeting & voting during the meeting.
4)	 For OTP based login you can click on https://eservices.nsdl.com/SecureWeb/evoting/
evotinglogin.jsp. You will have to enter your 8-digit DP ID,8-digit Client Id, PAN No., Verification 
code and generate OTP. Enter the OTP received on registered email id/mobile number and 
click on login. After successful authentication, you will be redirected to NSDL Depository site 
wherein you can see e-Voting page. Click on company name or e-Voting service provider 
name and you will be re-directed to e-Voting service provider website for casting your vote 
during the remote e-Voting period or joining virtual meeting & voting during the meeting.

meeting & voting during the meeting.

PAN

(i)	
After entering these details appropriately, click on 'SUBMIT' tab.

Shareholders holding shares in physical form will then directly reach the Company selection screen. However,

accordingly modify your vote.
(viii)	Once you 'CONFIRM' your vote on the resolution, you 
will not be allowed to modify your vote.

Item No. 4 Cost Auditor Remuneration
Pursuant to the provisions of Section 148(3) of the 
Companies Act, 2013 and Rule 14 of the Companies 
(Audit and Auditors) Rules, 2014, the Company is required 
to appoint a Cost Auditor to conduct the audit of the cost 
records of the applicable products of the Company. As per 
the Rules, the Board shall appoint an individual, who is a 
Cost Accountant, or a firm of Cost Accountants in practice, 
as cost auditor on the recommendations of the Audit 
Committee, which shall also recommend remuneration 
for such Cost Auditor. The remuneration recommended by 
the Audit Committee shall be considered and approved 
by the Board of Directors and ratified subsequently by 
the shareholders.
The Board of Directors of the Company, at its meeting held 
on August 11, 2026, had considered the recommendation 
of the Audit Committee and approved the appointment 
of M/s Geeyes & Co., Cost Accountant (Firm Registration 
No: 000044) as the Cost Auditor of the Company for the 
financial year 2026-27 at a remuneration of I5,50,000/- 
(Rupees Five lakhs fifty thousand only) plus applicable 
taxes and reimbursement of out-of-pocket expenses.
In accordance with the provisions of Section 148 of the Act 
read with the Companies (Cost Records and Audit) Rules, 
2014, the remuneration payable to the Cost Auditors is 
required to be ratified by the members of the Company. 
Accordingly, ratification by the members is sought to the 
remuneration payable to the Cost Auditors for the financial 
year ending March 31, 2027 by passing an Ordinary 
Resolution as set out at Item No. 4 of the Notice.
None of the Directors / Key Managerial Personnel of the 
Company / their relatives are, in any way, concerned or 
interested, financially or otherwise, in the resolution. The 
Board of directors accordingly recommends the Ordinary 
Resolution as set out at Item No.4 of the Notice for approval 
by the members.

13.	BOARD MEETINGS
	
During the year, four (4) meetings of the Board of 
Directors were held. The intervening gap between any 
two Meetings was less than 120 days in compliance 
with the provisions of Section 173(1) of the Companies 
Act, 2013. The complete details of the meetings are 
furnished in the Report on Corporate Governance 
which forms part of this Annual Report.
14.	MEETING OF INDEPENDENT DIRECTORS
	
During the year, one (1) separate meeting of 
Independent Directors was held. In the said meeting, 
the Independent Directors assessed the quality, 
quantity, and timeliness of flow of information between 
the management and the Board and have expressed 
that the current flow of information and contents 
were adequate for the Board to effectively perform 
its duties. They also reviewed the performance of 
the Non-Independent Directors and the Board as a 
whole and the performance of the Chairperson of the 
Company considering the views of Executive Director 
and Non- Executive Directors.
15.	BOARD DIVERSITY
	
The Company recognizes and embraces the 
importance of a diverse Board in contributing to its 
success. Adequate diversity on the Board is essential 
to meet the challenges of business globalization, rapid 
deployment of technology, greater social responsibility, 
increasing emphasis on corporate governance and 
enhanced need for risk management. The Board 
enables efficient functioning through differences 
in perspective and skill, and fosters differentiated 
thought processes at the back of varied industrial 
and management expertise, gender, knowledge, and 
geographical backgrounds. The Board has adopted the 
Board Diversity Policy which sets out the approach to 
diversity of the Board. The policy is available at the 
website of the Company at www.amrutanjan.com.
16.	 BOARD EVALUATION
	
Pursuant to Section 134 of the Companies Act, 2013 
read with Rule 8(4) of the Companies (Accounts) Rules, 
2014 and SEBI LODR, the Board of Directors during 
the year carried out an annual performance evaluation 
of its own performance, the Directors individually 
(without participation of the relevant Director) and 
the Chairman. The evaluation was carried through a 
structured methodology approved by the Nomination 
and Remuneration Committee.
	
The criteria for performance evaluation have been 
detailed in the Corporate Governance Report. The 
outcome of the evaluation also forms the basis for 
the Nomination and Remuneration Committee while 
considering the re-appointment of Directors.
17.	 REMUNERATION POLICY
	
The Remuneration policy of the Company deals with 
the appointment of and remuneration to the Executive 
& Non-Executive Directors, Key Managerial Personnel 
and Senior Executives of the Company including criteria 
for determining qualifications, positive attributes, 
independence of a Director and other related matters. 
It aims to attract, recruit and retain exceptional talent 
and ensures that the remuneration is performance 
driven. The Policy can be accessed in the website of the 
Company through the link – https://www.amrutanjan. 
com/policy.html. The Nomination and Remuneration 
Committee has also framed a Policy on Board 
diversity appropriate to the business requirements 
of the Company, which inter-alia specifies optimum 
combination of Executive Directors, Non-Executive 
Directors, and Independent Directors. The role of 
Nomination and Remuneration Committee is to ensure 
that the Policy on Board diversity is considered while 
recommending the appointment of new directors on 
the Board of the Company.
	
The details of remuneration paid/ payable to the 
Directors during the financial year 2025-26, are 
furnished in the Report on Corporate Governance 
annexed to this report.
18.	WHISTLE BLOWER MECHANISM
	
Pursuant to Section 177(9) of the Companies Act, 2013 
read with Rule 7 of the Companies (Meetings of Board 
and its Powers) Rules, 2014 and Regulation 22 of the 
SEBI (Listing Obligations & Disclosure Requirements) 
Regulations, 2015, the Board of Directors had 
approved the Policy on Vigil Mechanism/ Whistle 
Blower and the same was further amended effective 
from April 1, 2019 to comply with the amended SEBI 
Insider Trading Regulations. The Policy is hosted on 
the website of the Company https://www. amrutanjan.
com/pdf/Whistle%20Blower%20Policy. Pdf.
	
The Policy inter-alia provides direct access to the 
Chairman of the Audit Committee. Your Company 
hereby affirms that no Director/ employee has been 
denied access to the Chairman of the Audit Committee.
	
During the year, the policy has been updated with 
regard to the appointment of vigilant officer vide Board 
meeting dated 3 February 2026.

for a second term of five (5) years till the conclusion

statements forming part of the annual report.
	
With reference to clause 2B(f) of the Report, the 
Company has used SAP accounting software for 
maintaining its books of account which has a feature 
of recording audit trail (edit log) facility and there were 
no instances of tampering of audit trail for the period 
mentioned in the audit report. Additionally, where 
audit trail (audit log) was enabled and operated in the 
previous year(s), the audit trail has been preserved by 
the Company as per the statutory requirements for 
record retention. The desired standards were not met 
for few accounting softwares and hence the auditors 
were unable to check and comment on the same.
	
During the year, the statutory auditors have not 
reported to the Audit Committee any material fraud 
on the Company by its officers or employees under 
Section 143(12) of the Companies Act, 2013 requiring 
disclosure under Section 134(3) (ca) of the Companies 
Act, 2013.

21.	SECRETARIAL AUDITOR & AUDIT REPORT
	
Pursuant to the provisions of Section 204 of the 
Companies Act 2013, read with the Companies 
(Appointment and Remuneration of Managerial 
Personnel) Rules, 2014, the Company has appointed 
M/s SPNP & Associates, Practicing Company 
Secretaries, to conduct the secretarial audit of the 
Company for the financial year 2025-26.
	
The secretarial audit report certified by the Secretarial 
Auditor, in the specified form MR-3 is annexed herewith 
and forms part of this report as 'ANNEXURE E'.
	
Following observation was reported in the 
FY 2025-26:
	
It was observed that certain UPSI events were not 
contemporaneously recorded in the SDD; However, 
the same were subsequently updated as on the date 
of this Report. The instances include:
	
A.	
Appointment of Company Secretary.
	
B.	
Declarations of interim dividends dated 
November 10, 2025 and February 3, 2026.
	
In this regard, the Company had replied that: The 
Company was facing technical difficulties and 
as a result few events were not captured in the 
Structured Digital Database portal during this period. 
Subsequently, after rectification of the technical issue, 
the entries were duly captured in the Structured Digital 
Database and the Company ensured compliance with 
the requirements of Regulation 2(n) read with 3(5) of 
the SEBI (Prohibition of Insider Trading) Regulations, 
2015. Also during this period, the Trading Window 
was closed for all Designated Persons for the entire 
process and their PAN were also freezed, restricting 
them from trading during the relevant period. This 
ensured that the information remained protected 
completely and no unauthorised trading occurred.
	
Furthermore, pursuant to the amended provisions 
of Regulation 24A of the SEBI (Listing Obligations 
and Disclosure Requirements) Regulations, 2015 
and Section 204 of the Companies Act, 2013, read 
with Rule 9 of the Companies (Appointment and 
Remuneration of Managerial Personnel) Rules, 2014, 
the Audit Committee and the Board of Directors 
have approved and recommended the appointment 
of M/s. SPNP & Associates, Practicing Company 
Secretaries (Firm Registration Number: FR/CHENNAI 
CENTRAL/102/2020) as the Secretarial Auditors of 
the Company and approved in the Annual General 
Meeting held 23 September 2025, for a term of 5 (Five) 
consecutive financial years commencing from April 1, 
2025 till March 31, 2030, at such remuneration as 
may be determined by the Board of Directors of the 
Company (including its Committee thereof as may be 
authorized in this regard).

The Company has a proper and adequate internal 
control system to ensure that all the assets of the 
Company are safeguarded and protected against 
any loss and that all the transactions are properly 
authorised and recorded.
	
The internal control system is subject to continuous 
improvement, with system effectiveness, assessed 
regularly. Information provided to management is 
reliable and timely. Company ensures the reliability 
of financial reporting and compliance with laws and 
regulations. The details of the same are provided in 
the Management Discussion and Analysis Report.
30.	RISK MANAGEMENT POLICY
	
In accordance with the amended SEBI (Listing 
Obligations and Disclosure Requirements) Regulations, 
2015 effective from May 05, 2021, your Company 
has formed a Risk Management Committee and has 
put in place an enterprise wide Risk Management 
Framework with the objective of timely identification 
of risks, assessment and evaluation of such risks in line 
with the overall business objectives or strategies and 
define adequate mitigation strategy and adopted Risk 
Management Policy by the Board. Periodical in-house 
risk audits were conducted to detect and mitigate the 
risks in a timely and effective manner. Management 
Discussion and Analysis Report contain more details 
on the risk management practiced by the Company.
31.	 MATERIAL CHANGES AND COMMITMENTS 
AFFECTING THE FINANCIAL POSITION OF 
THE COMPANY

affecting the financial position of the Company during 
the year. Between the close of the financial year 2025-

the lease rent arrears totalling to Rs. 9,74,67,151/- 
(subject to deduction of tax at source) to Arulmigu 
Kapaleeswarar Temple, Mylapore, Chennai. With this 
payment, the matter with the temple is settled and no 
further dues are payable by the Company.

NOTE:
	
Subsequent to the close of the financial year ended 31 March 2026, the “Second Phase “ESOP 2” of the existing ESOP 
Scheme of the Company by the name of “Amrutanjan Health Care Limited – Employees Stock Option Scheme 2020” 
(Scheme”) was withdrawn and terminated with effect from 17 April 2026 due to non-achievement of sales targets 
and fall in the share price.
	
Thereafter, the Nomination and Remuneration Committee (Compensation Committee) approved the 
“revised ESOP 2” at the meeting held on 7 May 2026 and same was noted and recorded by the Board on the meeting 
held on 8 May 2026. The implementation of “revised ESOP 2” is as per the ESOP Scheme 2020 of the Company.
	
All unvested options were converted into options under the revised “ESOP 2” scheme on the terms ensuring no 
adverse impact on employees and preservation of intrinsic value and vesting benefits.
MANAGEMENT DISCUSSION & ANALYSIS 
Economic Overview
Global Economy
The global economy demonstrated resilience during 
FY2025–26 despite continued geopolitical uncertainties, 
evolving trade dynamics and persistent macroeconomic 
challenges. While growth moderated across several 
developed economies due to elevated interest rates and 
subdued consumer spending, easing inflationary pressures 
and improving financial conditions supported a gradual 
recovery in global economic activity.
According to the International Monetary Fund (IMF), global 
growth remained broadly stable, supported primarily by 
emerging and developing economies, which continued 
to contribute a significant share of incremental global 
GDP. Inflation continued to soften across most major 
economies as supply chain disruptions eased, commodity 
prices stabilized and central banks maintained prudent 
monetary policies. Nevertheless, policy rates in several 
advanced economies remained above historical averages, 
resulting in relatively tight financial conditions and cautious 
capital deployment.
Geopolitical developments, including continuing 
conflicts in Eastern Europe and the Middle East, together 
with evolving global trade policies and supply chain 
realignments, continued to influence energy prices, 
freight costs and business confidence. At the same time, 
companies across industries accelerated efforts to diversify 
sourcing, regionalize supply chains and strengthen 
operational resilience.
The global healthcare industry continued its transformation, 
driven by increasing consumer focus on preventive 
healthcare, digital health technologies and personalized 
wellness solutions. Adoption of artificial intelligence, 
connected healthcare, telemedicine and data-driven 
diagnostics continued to improve healthcare accessibility 
and efficiency across several markets. Consumers 
increasingly prioritized self-care and over-the-counter (OTC) 
healthcare solutions, creating favourable opportunities for 
companies operating in consumer healthcare categories.
Environmental sustainability remained a strategic priority 
globally, with businesses increasing investments in 
renewable energy, responsible sourcing, circular packaging 
and carbon reduction initiatives. These structural shifts are 
expected to continue shaping long-term business strategies 
across industries.
Overall, while macroeconomic uncertainties persist, the 
global economy remains supported by resilient labour 
markets, easing inflation and sustained investments 
in innovation, technology and healthcare, providing a 
constructive outlook for medium-term growth.
Indian Economy
India continued to outperform most major global 
economies during FY2025–26, supported by strong 
domestic consumption, sustained public infrastructure 
investments, healthy corporate balance sheets and 
continued policy reforms. The country's macroeconomic 
fundamentals remained robust despite external headwinds, 
reinforcing its position as one of the fastest-growing large 
economies globally.
Growth was supported by broad-based expansion across 
manufacturing, services and construction sectors, while 
rising private consumption and improving investment activity 
further strengthened economic momentum. Government 
initiatives focused on infrastructure development, 
manufacturing competitiveness, digitalization and ease 
of doing business continued to enhance India's long-term 
growth potential.
Inflation moderated during the year, supported by easing 
commodity prices and effective monetary policy measures. 
While food inflation remained subject to seasonal 
fluctuations, overall price stability improved, contributing 
to stronger consumer confidence and healthier spending 
patterns. India's
banking system remaine/d well capitalised, credit growth 
remained healthy and foreign exchange reserves continued 
to provide macroeconomic stability.
The Government's continued emphasis on capital 
expenditure, logistics infrastructure, digital public 
infrastructure and manufacturing incentives under 
various policy initiatives has significantly enhanced India's 
competitiveness as a global investment destination. Rapid 
urbanisation, increasing disposable incomes, favourable 
demographics and rising formalisation of the economy 
continue to support sustained consumption growth.
Digital adoption remains one of India's strongest structural 
growth drivers. Widespread internet penetration, rapid 
growth in digital payments and expanding e-commerce and 
quick commerce ecosystems are fundamentally changing 
consumer purchase behaviour across both urban and semi-
urban markets.
India's healthcare ecosystem is also witnessing structural 
transformation, driven by rising healthcare awareness, 
increasing health insurance penetration, expanding

## Page 28

healthcare infrastructure and greater consumer preference 
for preventive healthcare products. Growing demand for 
self-medication, wellness solutions and OTC healthcare 
products presents significant long-term opportunities for 
consumer healthcare companies.
Looking ahead, India's favourable demographic profile, 
rising consumption, ongoing infrastructure investments 
and policy continuity are expected to support sustainable 
economic growth, reinforcing its position as one of the 
world's most attractive long-term growth markets.
Industry Structure and Development 
Industry Overview
The Fast-Moving Consumer Goods (FMCG) industry 
continued to demonstrate resilience during FY2025–26 
despite a dynamic operating environment characterised 
by inflationary pressures, changing consumer preferences 
and evolving retail formats. The sector benefited from 
improving rural demand, stable urban consumption and 
continued expansion of organised retail, e-commerce and 
quick commerce platforms.
Consumer spending remained strong, supported by rising 
disposable incomes, favourable employment conditions 
and increasing aspirations across both urban and rural 
India. Premiumisation continued across several FMCG 
categories as consumers increasingly sought products 
offering superior quality, convenience, health benefits and 
enhanced consumer experience.
Health and wellness emerged as one of the strongest 
long-term consumption trends. Consumers are increasingly 
prioritising preventive healthcare, immunity enhancement, 
natural ingredients and scientifically backed formulations. 
This shift continues to drive demand across OTC healthcare 
products, functional foods, nutraceuticals and wellness-
focused personal care categories.
Digital transformation is fundamentally reshaping the 
FMCG landscape. Companies are leveraging data analytics, 
artificial intelligence, automation and digital consumer 
engagement to improve product innovation, supply chain 
efficiency and marketing effectiveness. Digital commerce—
including e-commerce, quick commerce and omnichannel 
retail—has become an integral growth engine, enabling 
wider product availability, faster fulfilment and deeper 
consumer engagement.
Quick commerce has emerged as a particularly important 
channel for consumer healthcare products, especially in 
urban markets where immediacy of need significantly 
influences purchase decisions. Products addressing 
everyday ailments, wellness and convenience continue to 
witness increasing adoption across digital platforms.
Sustainability continues to gain strategic importance 
across the FMCG value chain. Companies are increasingly 
investing in responsible sourcing, recyclable packaging, 
resource-efficient manufacturing and carbon footprint 
reduction to meet evolving consumer expectations and 
regulatory requirements. These initiatives are expected 
to strengthen brand equity while creating long-term 
operational efficiencies.
The Indian FMCG sector also benefited from improving rural 
demand during the year, supported by better agricultural 
output, favourable monsoon conditions, government 
welfare programmes and higher rural income levels. Rural 
markets are gradually narrowing the growth gap with urban 
markets, providing incremental opportunities for category 
expansion and household penetration.
The consumer healthcare segment continues to benefit 
from increasing awareness regarding self-care, preventive 
healthcare and early symptom management. Consumers 
are increasingly seeking convenient, accessible and trusted 
OTC solutions for common health conditions, supported 
by greater digital awareness and improved product 
accessibility across modern trade, pharmacies, e-commerce 
and quick commerce channels.
While the industry continues to face periodic volatility 
arising from raw material prices, competitive intensity 
and evolving regulatory requirements, its long-term 
fundamentals remain strong. Rising healthcare awareness, 
favourable demographics, increasing digital adoption, 
expanding organised retail and sustained innovation are 
expected to continue supporting healthy growth across the 
FMCG and consumer healthcare sectors over the medium 
to long term.
Implications of Economic Overview on AHCL:
While global macroeconomic uncertainties continue to 
influence commodity prices, freight and supply-chain costs, 
your Company remains focused on improving sourcing, 
manufacturing and supply-chain efficiencies, accelerating 
digitisation and maintaining disciplined cost management. 
At the same time, the global shift towards preventive care 
and self-care supports the long-term opportunity for the 
Company’s health and hygiene portfolio.
India’s strong consumption outlook, increasing healthcare 
awareness, expanding digital infrastructure and growing 
organised retail provide a supportive foundation for the 
Company’s long-term growth strategy. These trends 
reinforce its focus on strengthening Amrutanjan and Comfy, 
expanding distribution beyond traditional strong markets, 
increasing participation across e-commerce and quick 
commerce, and investing in consumer-led innovation. With 
trusted brands and expanding execution capabilities, your 
Company remains confident of delivering sustainable and 
profitable growth.
The shift towards self-care, preventive health and accessible 
OTC solutions directly supports AHCL’s strategy across 
pain management, women’s hygiene and rehydration. 
Your Company is positioned to benefit through portfolio 
expansion, consumer education, wider availability across 
chemists, traditional trade and digital commerce, and 
continued investment in manufacturing and supply-chain 
capabilities. While regulatory change, input-cost volatility 
and competitive intensity require disciplined execution, 
trusted brands, expanding distribution and innovation 
provide a strong foundation for long-term value creation.
Category Overview
The *Rubefacients category continued its growth path 
in FY26, with both value and volume recording healthy 
growth, indicating that the category expansion was 
primarily consumption-led. The category was valued at 
I8,229 crore in FY26. AHCL outperformed the category 
and other major players in terms of volume growth, while 
its value and volume growth remained broadly aligned. This 
reflects strong underlying consumer demand and reinforces 
the Company’s focus on driving sustainable, consumption-
led growth.

Within the Rubefacients category, the Headache segment 
maintained a growth trajectory in FY26, broadly in line with 
the previous year, across value and volume. The category 
grew from I1,929 crore in FY25 to I2,014 crore in FY26. 
AHCL continued to deliver growth during the year, with 
Amrutanjan Headache recording volume growth ahead 
of the overall headache category, reflecting continued 
consumer preference and strengthening brand momentum.
The Body Ache segment was valued at I4,173 crore in 
FY26 and delivered near-double-digit value growth over 
a higher base in FY25. While AHCL operates from a 
relatively smaller base in this segment, its volume growth 
was significantly ahead of the category across all quarters 
of FY26, indicating increasing consumer acceptance of the 
Company's Body Ache portfolio.
These positive category trends were also reflected in 
the Company's internal sales performance, with strong 
secondary sales growth across the Headache and Body 
Ache segments, as well as the overall Rubefacients 
portfolio. This momentum translated into near 
double-digit growth in primary sales for the Rubefacients 
category, reinforcing AHCL's strong market presence and 
continued growth momentum.

Company Overview 
OTC Business
The Company’s Over-the-Counter (OTC) segment delivered 
gross sales of I518.6 crore in FY26, maintaining a strong 
growth performance during the year. General Trade (GT) 
continued to be the mainstay of the segment, contributing 
84.4% of total OTC turnover and registering a healthy 
12% growth over FY25. This performance was led by the 
Chemists channel, the Company’s largest contributor, 
where AHCL recorded volume growth 1.8 times that of 
the Headache category, underscoring the strength of the 
Company’s brands and sustained consumer demand.
The Company continued to strengthen its presence 
across emerging and high-growth channels, with focused

Marketing Activities
STRATEGIC APPROACH Building salience at scale while 
creating category-relevant experiences at the point of 
viewing, purchase and product trial.
During FY 2025-26, Amrutanjan continued to strengthen 
its market presence through an integrated marketing 
approach combining brand communication, strategic 
media investments, retail visibility and consumer-centric 
activations. The programme was designed to keep the 
Company's trusted propositions visible across the consumer 
journey—from mass and regional media to neighbourhood 
retail, workplaces, public gatherings and emerging 
rural markets.
The year's interventions were organised around the distinct 
growth requirements of five business priorities: Pain 
Management, Congestion, Women's Hygiene, Beverages 
and Rural Expansion. Within each category, communication 
was linked to a clear role. Television and high-impact 
properties built broad awareness; in-store visibility 
supported consideration at the point of purchase; sampling 
enabled first-hand experience; and rural vans strengthened 
last-mile access and retailer coverage.
New launches were supported within their natural category 
context. Advanced Ortho Pain Relief Oil extended the Body 
portfolio; Relief Nasal Spray broadened the congestion 
range; Comfy Slimfit XXL Night Pads addressed the fast-
growing night-use segment; and Comfy Women's Razors 
expanded the personal-care adjacency of the Comfy 
franchise. This category-led approach enabled innovation 
to benefit from the trust, media support and retail presence 
of the relevant portfolio.
•	 Pain - Head & Body: Refreshed communication, high-
impact media, in-store visibility, sampling and Advanced 
Ortho Pain Relief Oil.
•	 Congestion: Integrated television and digital 
communication, range visibility and Relief Nasal Spray.
•	 Women's Hygiene: Sustained TV support, retail 
activation, education-led sampling, Comfy Night Pads 
and Women's Razors.
•	 Beverages: Electro+; Enerlyte: consumer education, 
retail visibility, digital influence and large-scale 
product trial.
•	 Rural Expansion: Van-led retailer and distributor 
outreach across small towns and villages.
The Headache segment continued to anchor AHCL’s 
domestic business in FY26, generating gross sales of 
I282.04 crore and contributing 55.6% to the Company’s 
total domestic revenue of I507.45 crore. While the segment 
continued to be the largest contributor to the portfolio, its 
share moderated during the year, reflecting the increasing 
contribution of the Company’s diversified portfolio and the 
strong growth delivered by its emerging categories.
AHCL’s multi-category, multi-brand strategy continued to 
gain momentum during the year. The Women’s Hygiene 
(WH) category delivered a strong performance, with its 
contribution to the overall portfolio increasing from 28% 
to 30%. This growth reflects the Company’s sustained 
investments in the category and its efforts to build scale 
and strengthen consumer relevance.
Comfy, AHCL’s flagship Women’s Hygiene brand and the 
second-largest contributor to the portfolio, continued its 
strong growth momentum, registering an 18% increase in 
revenue in FY26 over FY25. The WH category achieved a 
significant milestone during the year, with gross revenue 
reaching I151.31 crore, compared with I127.79 crore in the 
previous financial year.
The Body category further strengthened its position as the 
third-largest contributor to the OTC portfolio, recording 
sales of I50.29 crore in FY26. At the same time, the 
Company’s entry into the Personal Care category began 
contributing to overall revenues, marking another step 
forward in AHCL’s journey towards building a broader and 
more diversified portfolio. These developments underscore 
the Company’s continued progress in expanding beyond 
its core categories, creating new growth engines, and 
strengthening its position as a multi-category, multi-brand 
consumer healthcare organisation.

Rehydration Category
Climate change is contributing to a rise in the frequency 
and intensity of extreme-heat events globally, making 
elevated temperatures an increasingly persistent 
feature of the environment. The World Meteorological 
Organization (WMO) confirmed that 2025 was the second 
or third warmest year on record, with the global average 
temperature approximately 1.43°C above the 1850–1900 
pre-industrial average. In India, the annual mean land-
surface air temperature in 2025 was 0.28°C above the 
1991–2020 long-term average, making it the eighth 
warmest year since records began in 1901.
Prolonged exposure to high temperatures places additional 
stress on the human body, increasing perspiration and 
the loss of fluids and essential electrolytes. Inadequate 
replacement of these losses can result in dehydration and 
associated symptoms such as fatigue, weakness, dizziness, 
muscle cramps, and reduced physical and cognitive 
performance. Severe heat exposure may also lead to 
heat exhaustion and heatstroke, while extreme heat can 
aggravate certain cardiovascular, respiratory, renal, and 
metabolic health conditions.
Against this backdrop, maintaining adequate hydration 
is becoming increasingly important for a broad range of 
consumers, including outdoor workers, athletes, commuters, 
students, travellers, and individuals leading active lifestyle. 
While water remains fundamental to everyday hydration, 
situations involving prolonged heat exposure, intense 
physical activity, or excessive sweating may increase the 
need to replenish both fluids and electrolytes to support 
the restoration of the body's fluid balance.
Amrutanjan recognises the growing relevance of hydration 
as a consumer health need and the opportunity to support 
consumers through appropriate hydration solutions. The 
evolving climate landscape, changing lifestyles, and 
increasing awareness of preventive health are expected to 
further strengthen consumer focus on hydration, wellness, 
and everyday health management, creating opportunities for 
the Company to innovate and address emerging consumer 
needs through relevant and responsible solutions.
Rehydration New Launch: Meeting the Diverse Needs 
of Consumers
As a consumer driven organisation, AHCL remains 
committed to addressing the evolving health and wellness 
needs of consumers through relevant, science-backed 
solutions. Recognising that rehydration needs vary across 
consumer groups and their usage occasions, the Company 
has adopted a differentiated portfolio approach. With the 
launch of Enerlyte, alongside the existing Electro+ range 
available in Tetra and Powder formats, AHCL has further 
strengthened its presence in the rehydration category and 
expanded its ability to address diverse consumer needs.

Enerlyte has been introduced to address the growing 
need for everyday hydration. In the context of India's hot 
climate and increasingly active lifestyles, consumers may 
experience fluid and electrolyte loss due to heat exposure, 
travel, work, and physical activity. While plain water 
remains fundamental to daily hydration, consumers often 
have limited options between conventional beverages and 
therapeutic rehydration solutions. Enerlyte is positioned 
to address this space by offering a convenient and 
science-backed hydration solution suited to everyday 
consumption occasions.
With the introduction of Enerlyte and the continued 
availability of Electro+ in Tetra and Powder formats, AHCL 
has further broadened its rehydration portfolio to address 
the distinct rehydration needs of different consumers. This 
portfolio expansion reinforces the Company's commitment 
to delivering accessible, science-backed health and 
wellness solutions while strengthening its position in the 
evolving rehydration category.

Women’s Hygiene: Strengthening Presence and 
Driving Inclusive Growth
Menstrual hygiene continues to be an important public 
health priority in India, with sanitary napkin penetration 
remaining below 50% and significantly higher in urban 
areas than in rural markets (Urban is 1.6 times of Rural). 
In rural India, many women continue to rely on traditional 
alternatives such as cloth, driven by factors including limited 
access, affordability constraints, and gaps in awareness. 
This highlights a significant opportunity to expand the 
category and improve access to affordable menstrual 
hygiene solutions, particularly across underserved markets.
Comfy Sanitary Napkins, AHCL’s flagship brand in 
the Women’s Hygiene segment, continues to focus on 
addressing this opportunity by offering affordable, quality 
menstrual hygiene solutions designed to meet the needs 
of rural and lower-income urban consumers. The brand's 
predominantly rural outlet presence reflects its commitment 
to expanding access and promoting inclusive menstrual 
hygiene adoption.
Comfy delivered strong double-digit sales growth of 19% 
in FY26 over FY25, with the XL variant recording significant 
growth of 44%, reflecting evolving consumer preferences 
and increasing adoption of longer-length pads.
The brand also continued to strengthen its consumer 
franchise, with key brand health indicators—including 
awareness, consideration, usage and satisfaction of the 
brand (among users) —showing a consistent upward 
trajectory over the years. This sustained improvement 
reflects growing consumer acceptance of Comfy and 
reinforces the brand’s increasing relevance in supporting 
better menstrual hygiene practices among women 
across India.
Period Care Range

Rural Expansion: Reaching the Last-Mile Retailer
The rural van initiative combined portfolio promotion with distributor and sub-distributor development.
Amrutanjan continued to expand its presence in small towns 
and villages through dedicated rural van initiatives. The 
programme had two connected objectives: to widen rural 
market coverage for the AHCL portfolio and to strengthen 
the distributor and sub-distributor network required for 
sustained availability.
The van activity covered Uttar Pradesh, Tamil Nadu, 
Maharashtra, Gujarat, Madhya Pradesh, Chhattisgarh, 
Jharkhand, Andhra Pradesh and Telangana. Mobile assets 
carried portfolio communication into underserved markets, 
supporting retailer engagement, product awareness and 
promotion closer to the point of sale.
Approximately 1,000 villages were reached through rural 
outreach, introducing the product range to previously 
untapped consumers. These villages were identified for 
transition into regular coverage areas, linking activation 
with a longer-term route-to-market objective rather than 
treating the van programme as a one-time event.
The initiative also supported last-mile visibility by enabling 
the Company to engage retailers directly, demonstrate the 
breadth of the portfolio and strengthen local distribution 
relationships. Pain Management benefited from the rural 
outreach, with its contribution to AHCL revenues increasing 
in FY26.

MARKETING ACTIVITIES:
An Integrated System for Sustainable Brand 
Growth
Across FY 2025-26, Amrutanjan's marketing activities 
were anchored in a simple principle: each category requires 
a distinct consumer story, but growth is strongest when 
communication, trial, retail visibility and availability work 
together. The year's programme therefore balanced mass 
reach with category-specific execution.
From awareness to experience
Television and high-impact entertainment properties built 
broad salience for Head & Body and Comfy. Regional 
television and digital communication made Relief more 
contemporary and relevant to everyday congestion 
occasions. Influencer content placed Electro+ within a 
daily rehydration context. Across categories, the role of 
communication remained disciplined: establish the problem, 
demonstrate the proposition and make the product easier 
to remember.
From experience to consideration
Sampling at pilgrimage destinations, festivals, workplaces, 
schools, marathons, high-footfall locations and through 
quick commerce enabled first-hand product engagement. 
Education material added context where the category 
required it, particularly for menstrual hygiene and 
rehydration. This combination supported informed trial 
rather than product distribution alone.
From consideration to availability
Shop boards, posters, standees, hanger packs, parasite 
placements, flanges and counter-top units strengthened 
visibility close to purchase. Rural vans expanded this 
principle beyond established retail, supporting distributors, 
sub-distributors and retailers across the states covered 
and creating a pathway from activation to regular 
market coverage.
Innovation within trusted category platforms
Advanced Ortho Pain Relief Oil, Relief Nasal Spray, Comfy 
Slimfit XXL Night Pads and Comfy Women's Razors 
were integrated within the categories whose trust and 
infrastructure could best support them. By connecting 
innovation to established category stories, Amrutanjan 
strengthened portfolio breadth while retaining the 
familiarity and reliability at the heart of the brand.
Modern Trade (MT)
The Modern Trade (MT) channel continued its growth trend in FY 26, clocking a revenue of 52.59 Crs when compared to 
I51.32 Crs during the previous financial year. Over the last 13 years, the MT channel has expanded over nine-fold, achieving 
an impressive Compound Annual Growth Rate (CAGR) of 19.7%.
This consistent performance underscores the success of AHCL’s strategic initiatives and the increasing consumer demand 
for its portfolio in the modern retail environment. The Company remains focused on deepening its presence in this high-
potential channel by leveraging consumer insights and collaborative retail partnerships.

Strengthening Distribution Reach and Market 
Accessibility
AHCL continued to strengthen its distribution footprint 
during FY26, expanding the availability of its products 
to 1.12 million retail outlets across India. This extensive 
distribution network enables the Company to enhance 
product accessibility across diverse town classes and 
markets, supporting its commitment to addressing 
the evolving healthcare and wellness needs of 
consumers nationwide.
As part of its focused distribution strategy, the Company 
undertook a time-bound, targeted initiative to expand its 
presence across chemist outlets, one of its key channels. 
The initiative resulted in a meaningful increase in chemist 
outlet coverage, further strengthening AHCL’s market 
reach and improving the availability of its products at key 
consumer touchpoints.

Digital Acceleration: Expanding Reach Through 
E-commerce and Quick Commerce
AHCL’s continued focus on strengthening its digital 
presence delivered strong results in FY26, with the 
Company’s products becoming increasingly accessible 
across leading e-commerce and digital platforms. The 
Company expanded its presence across platforms including 
Amazon D2C, City Mall, DMART ECOM, Flipkart, Netmeds, 
PharmEasy, SastaSundar, Tata 1mg, BigBasket B2C, Blinkit, 
Flipkart Hyperlocal, Swiggy, and Zepto. As a result, the 
E-commerce (ECOM) channel recorded revenue of I14.12 
crore in FY26, registering robust growth of 48.8% over the 
previous year.
A significant highlight during the year was the rapid 
expansion of the Quick Commerce (QCOM) channel, 
reinforcing AHCL’s strategy of enhancing consumer 
convenience and enabling faster access to its products. 
The Company strengthened its partnerships with leading 
platforms, including Blinkit, Swiggy Instamart, Zepto, and 
Flipkart Hyperlocal, enabling consumers to access select 
products within minutes and supporting the Company's 
proposition of delivering quick and effective relief.
The QCOM channel emerged as the fastest-growing channel 
for the Company, recording revenue of I7.55 crore in FY26, 
representing a robust 84.2% growth over the previous year. 
This strong performance reflects the increasing relevance 
of convenience-led consumption and the growing adoption 
of digital-first purchase occasions.
AHCL remains committed to accelerating its digital 
transformation and strengthening its presence across 
ECOM and QCOM platforms. Continued investments in 
these channels will focus on expanding consumer reach, 
enhancing accessibility and convenience, and creating 
sustainable growth opportunities for the Company.
Exports: 
Expanding 
Global 
Presence 
and 
Strengthening Export Growth
AHCL’s Export business delivered its strongest performance 
in the last 13 years, recording revenue of I11.15 crore in 
FY26, representing an impressive 36.1% growth over the 
previous year. The Export business has maintained a strong 
growth trajectory, registering a CAGR of 18.1% over the 
past 13 years, reflecting the Company’s sustained efforts 
to build and expand its international presence.
Africa continued to be the largest contributor to export 
revenue, accounting for 81% of total exports in FY26. 
While the contribution from the Middle East and 
Southeast Asia moderated during the year, revenues 
from other international markets doubled compared with 
the previous year, indicating encouraging progress in 
geographical diversification.
From a category perspective, the Head segment regained 
momentum during FY26, contributing 45% of total export 
revenue. The Body segment continued to be the leading 
category, contributing 46% and remaining marginally ahead 
of the Head segment.
The strong performance of the Export business reflects 
AHCL’s continued focus on expanding its geographical 
footprint, diversifying its international market presence, and 
strengthening its category portfolio across global markets. 
These efforts are expected to support the Company in 
building a more diversified and sustainable international 
growth platform.

As part of our global growth strategy, Amrutanjan Healthcare Limited launched Amrutanjan Relief in the United States 
through Amazon US in FY 25-26, marking a significant milestone in the Company's international expansion journey.
The launch introduced Amrtanjan's trusted pain relief expertise to the world's largest consumer healthcare market, with 
a portfolio of topical pain relief products designed to address muscle aches, joint pain and everyday body discomfort. 
Leveraging over 130 years of heritage, trusted formulations and consumer confidence built over generations, the brand 
aims to establish a strong presence in the US OTC healthcare category.
The initial launch through Amazon provides a scalable digital-first platform to build brand awareness, generate consumer 
adoption and gain valuable market insights This initiative reflects Amrutanjan Healthcare's long-term vision of building a 
global consumer healthcare business while delivering sustainable growth and creating long-term value for its shareholders.

Manufacturing
Amrutanjan Health Care Ltd (AHCL) continues to be a cornerstone of India’s Over-the-Counter (OTC) healthcare landscape. 
Founded in 1893, the Company has evolved from a single-product entity (the iconic yellow pain balm) into a diversified 
consumer goods player with a significant presence in pain management, congestion relief, beverages, and feminine 
hygiene. Guided by a century-old commitment to wellness, the Company continues to innovate and grow its presence, 
both domestically and internationally.
Our OTC manufacturing units are certified to ISO 9001 Quality Management System standards and fully adhere to Good 
Manufacturing Practices and the regulations outlined in the Drugs and Cosmetics Act. We uphold the highest quality 
standards during manufacturing to ensure effective, defect-free products. With a capacity of 1,800 MT, we are well-
equipped to meet market demand.
To expand our global presence, we have established a new Allopathic division, supported by a cGMP-certified manufacturing 
facility. This division is dedicated to the production of OTC monograph topical application products intended for export to 
the US and other regulated markets.

Women Hygiene
In Feminine Care, we have commissioned our Greenfield 
Sanitary Napkin Manufacturing Facility at Seetharampur 
Village, Shabad Mandal, Rangareddy District, Telangana, 
approximately 45 km from Hyderabad. The I150 crore 
investment marks a major milestone in the Company's 
manufacturing journey and strengthens its commitment to 
delivering high-quality feminine hygiene products under 
the Comfy brand.
Spread across 10 acres with a 1.40 lakh sq. ft. built-up 
area, the facility is AHCL's fifth manufacturing plant and 
has been strategically located to leverage Hyderabad's 
strong industrial ecosystem, excellent road connectivity, 
skilled workforce, and efficient access to key markets 
across India. The project also contributes to the economic 
development of the surrounding rural communities by 
creating sustainable employment opportunities.
Designed as a state-of-the-art manufacturing facility, the 
plant features two fully automated high-speed production 
lines from Japan, making it one of India's fastest sanitary 
napkin manufacturing facilities. With a planned annual 
production capacity of 700 million sanitary napkins, the plant 
combines advanced automation, precision manufacturing, 
and robust quality systems to ensure consistent product 
quality and operational excellence.
The facility is expected to generate 200 direct and 150 
indirect employment opportunities, supporting local talent 
while strengthening the regional manufacturing ecosystem.
Sustainability has been integrated into the plant's design 
through rainwater harvesting, an on-site Sewage Treatment 
Plant (STP), waste recycling systems, and energy-efficient 
LED lighting. The Company also plans to incorporate solar 
power in the future, reinforcing its long-term commitment 
to responsible manufacturing.
This investment reflects AHCL’s vision of building future-
ready manufacturing capabilities, enabling the ‘Make in 
India’ initiative. By combining advanced technology, large-
scale production capacity, sustainable practices, and local 
employment generation, the Telangana facility is well-
positioned to meet the growing demand for quality feminine 
hygiene products and strengthen AHCL's leadership in 
the sector.
Research & Development, Quality, and Safety
Our Research and Development team adheres to a Quality 
by Design (QbD) approach in the development of new 
products that are both efficacious and provide significant 
value to our customers. Our flagship products have 
undergone comprehensive clinical studies, which validate 
their efficacy and safety. Furthermore, we actively explore 
innovative technologies in the design of our packaging to 
enhance our competitive advantage. The other initiatives 
of R&D include
a.	
Enhancement of existing products in terms of Quality 
and Efficacy.
b.	
Collaborating with alternate suppliers to mitigate 
single vendor risk.
c.
